        Idea:
Build a lightweight model benchmarking harness that tests multiple LLM models across megaplan phases to find the best model for each role.

Goal: Run 3 representative evals (simple, medium, hard) through each of ~10 models, scoring each model on each phase independently, to produce a matrix of model×phase scores.

What to build:

1. A benchmark runner (evals/benchmark.py) that:
   - Takes a list of models and a list of eval names
   - For each model, runs it through each phase type independently (not full pipeline):
     a. PLAN test: Give the model a plan prompt for each eval. Score: did it return valid JSON with required keys? Did it use web search? Quality of plan (has success criteria, assumptions, concrete steps)?
     b. CRITIQUE test: Give it a plan to critique. Score: valid JSON? Found real issues vs zero flags?
     c. EXECUTE test: Give it a finalized task list. Score: did it use write_file/patch to modify code? Did the build pass? Did it produce valid task_updates JSON?
     d. REVIEW test: Give it execution evidence. Score: valid JSON with verdict and task_verdicts?
   - Each test is independent (not a full pipeline) — we're measuring per-phase capability
   - Tests run in parallel across models (concurrent subprocess calls)
   - Results stored as a matrix: model × phase × eval → score

2. Scoring dimensions per phase:
   - json_valid: 0/1 — did the response parse as valid JSON matching the schema?
   - json_in_content: 0/1 — was the JSON in the content field (not reasoning/think tags)?
   - required_keys: 0/1 — does it have all required keys?
   - tool_use: 0/1 — did it use appropriate tools (file read for plan/critique, file write for execute)?
   - web_search: 0/1 — did it use web_search (bonus for plan/critique)?
   - build_pass: 0/1 — execute only: does npm run build succeed?
   - eval_pass: 0/1 — execute only: do EVAL.ts assertions pass?

3. Models to test (all via OpenRouter):
   - minimax/minimax-m2.7
   - z-ai/glm-5
   - anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6
   - openai/gpt-4.1-mini
   - google/gemini-2.5-flash
   - meta-llama/llama-4-maverick
   - deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3-0324
   - qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b
   - mistralai/mistral-medium-3
   - cohere/command-a

4. Evals to use:
   - Simple: agent-025-prefer-next-link (add 3 Link components)
   - Medium: agent-022-prefer-server-actions (convert to server action pattern)
   - Hard: agent-000-app-router-migration-simple (full Pages→App Router migration)

5. Output: 
   - Console table showing model × phase scores
   - JSON file with full results for analysis
   - Recommendation of best model per phase

6. Parallelism:
   - Run all models concurrently (each in its own subprocess)
   - Each model runs sequentially through the 3 evals × 4 phases = 12 tests
   - Total: ~120 model-phase tests, but only 10 concurrent at a time

The harness should reuse the existing hermes worker infrastructure but bypass the full megaplan pipeline — it calls the hermes worker directly for each phase test with pre-prepared prompts and scores the result.

    "DEBT: scoring-diffing \u2014 git diff HEAD~1 fails when workspace has only one initial commit. Use `git diff <initial_sha>..HEAD` instead.",
    "DEBT: trace-collection \u2014 sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so reads return cumulative history. Use message_offset from PhaseRecord to isolate phase-specific messages.",
    "DEBT: worker-dispatch \u2014 plan's sample function signatures may not match actual worker signatures. Verify against evals/run_evals.py before coding.",
    "DECISION-001: Workspace paths MUST include model slug: workspace_dir/<model.replace('/', '--')>/<eval_name> to prevent parallel clobbering.",
    "DECISION-002: json_in_content is DROPPED from scoring \u2014 raw provider responses aren't captured (PhaseScore has 6 dimensions, not 7).",
    "DECISION-003: Execute phase starts from clean prepared workspace + finalize.json with NO pre-applied reference diff. Review phase DOES apply the diff.",
    "prepare_workspace() at evals/run_evals.py:255-258 unconditionally deletes and recreates destination \u2014 must wrap with model slug path before calling.",
    "_session_key_for() appends sha256[:8] of model name \u2014 benchmark must use same function (import from evals.run_evals) to generate matching keys.",

  "meta_commentary": "Critical execution guidance:\n\n**Fixture generation (T1) is the bottleneck.** Everything else can be coded without fixtures, but T5 and T9 cannot run until real fixtures exist. If you don't have existing successful eval runs to harvest from, you'll need to run 3 full pipelines with Claude Opus first \u2014 budget ~30-60min and real API costs for this.\n\n**State bootstrapping is the riskiest part.** The FakeMegaplanRunner at tests/test_evals/test_run_evals.py:42-57 is the authoritative reference. Key gotchas: (1) _session_key_for() appends a sha256[:8] hash of the model name \u2014 you MUST import this function from evals.run_evals, not reimplement it. (2) State.json lives inside `.megaplan/plans/<plan_name>/` not at repo root. (3) Session files go to $HERMES_HOME/sessions/ \u2014 set HERMES_HOME per-model to avoid collisions.\n\n**HERMES_HOME isolation is critical for parallelism.** Each concurrent model needs its own HERMES_HOME so session files don't collide. Pass this as env var to the megaplan subprocess. The FakeMegaplanRunner reads `os.environ['HERMES_HOME']` directly (line 126).\n\n**prepare_workspace() will clobber parallel runs** if called with the same workspace_dir \u2014 it does `shutil.rmtree(destination)` at line 256-257. The model slug in the path (DECISION-001) only helps if you construct `workspace_dir/<model_slug>` BEFORE calling prepare_workspace, so the eval_name subdirectory is unique per model.\n\n**Execute scoring depends on npm install completing** \u2014 prepare_workspace runs `npm install` + `npm install next@canary` which can take minutes. For the execute phase, this is unavoidable since you need build_pass and eval_pass. For plan/critique/review, consider whether you actually need a fully prepared workspace or just the .megaplan directory structure.\n\n**Fallback strategy:** If synthetic state bootstrapping proves too brittle for a phase, fall back to running `megaplan init` + prerequisite phases with a cheap model (e.g., gpt-4.1-mini), then swap to the target model for the target phase only. This is slower but guaranteed to produce valid state.\n\n**T2, T3, T4, T7 can all be coded in parallel** since they have no dependencies on each other. T8 depends on T2+T3 being done. T5 depends on all of T1-T4. T6 depends on T5. T9 is the final integration gate."
}

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Build a lightweight model benchmarking harness that tests multiple LLM models across megaplan phases to find the best model for each role.

Goal: Run 3 representative evals (simple, medium, hard) through each of ~10 models, scoring each model on each phase independently, to produce a matrix of model×phase scores.

What to build:

1. A benchmark runner (evals/benchmark.py) that:
   - Takes a list of models and a list of eval names
   - For each model, runs it through each phase type independently (not full pipeline):
     a. PLAN test: Give the model a plan prompt for each eval. Score: did it return valid JSON with required keys? Did it use web search? Quality of plan (has success criteria, assumptions, concrete steps)?
     b. CRITIQUE test: Give it a plan to critique. Score: valid JSON? Found real issues vs zero flags?
     c. EXECUTE test: Give it a finalized task list. Score: did it use write_file/patch to modify code? Did the build pass? Did it produce valid task_updates JSON?
     d. REVIEW test: Give it execution evidence. Score: valid JSON with verdict and task_verdicts?
   - Each test is independent (not a full pipeline) — we're measuring per-phase capability
   - Tests run in parallel across models (concurrent subprocess calls)
   - Results stored as a matrix: model × phase × eval → score

2. Scoring dimensions per phase:
   - json_valid: 0/1 — did the response parse as valid JSON matching the schema?
   - json_in_content: 0/1 — was the JSON in the content field (not reasoning/think tags)?
   - required_keys: 0/1 — does it have all required keys?
   - tool_use: 0/1 — did it use appropriate tools (file read for plan/critique, file write for execute)?
   - web_search: 0/1 — did it use web_search (bonus for plan/critique)?
   - build_pass: 0/1 — execute only: does npm run build succeed?
   - eval_pass: 0/1 — execute only: do EVAL.ts assertions pass?

3. Models to test (all via OpenRouter):
   - minimax/minimax-m2.7
   - z-ai/glm-5
   - anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6
   - openai/gpt-4.1-mini
   - google/gemini-2.5-flash
   - meta-llama/llama-4-maverick
   - deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3-0324
   - qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b
   - mistralai/mistral-medium-3
   - cohere/command-a

4. Evals to use:
   - Simple: agent-025-prefer-next-link (add 3 Link components)
   - Medium: agent-022-prefer-server-actions (convert to server action pattern)
   - Hard: agent-000-app-router-migration-simple (full Pages→App Router migration)

5. Output: 
   - Console table showing model × phase scores
   - JSON file with full results for analysis
   - Recommendation of best model per phase

6. Parallelism:
   - Run all models concurrently (each in its own subprocess)
   - Each model runs sequentially through the 3 evals × 4 phases = 12 tests
   - Total: ~120 model-phase tests, but only 10 concurrent at a time

The harness should reuse the existing hermes worker infrastructure but bypass the full megaplan pipeline — it calls the hermes worker directly for each phase test with pre-prepared prompts and scores the result.

    "DEBT: scoring-diffing \u2014 git diff HEAD~1 fails when workspace has only one initial commit. Use `git diff <initial_sha>..HEAD` instead.",
    "DEBT: trace-collection \u2014 sessions are reused across megaplan phases, so reads return cumulative history. Use message_offset from PhaseRecord to isolate phase-specific messages.",
    "DEBT: worker-dispatch \u2014 plan's sample function signatures may not match actual worker signatures. Verify against evals/run_evals.py before coding.",
    "DECISION-001: Workspace paths MUST include model slug: workspace_dir/<model.replace('/', '--')>/<eval_name> to prevent parallel clobbering.",
    "DECISION-002: json_in_content is DROPPED from scoring \u2014 raw provider responses aren't captured (PhaseScore has 6 dimensions, not 7).",
    "DECISION-003: Execute phase starts from clean prepared workspace + finalize.json with NO pre-applied reference diff. Review phase DOES apply the diff.",
    "prepare_workspace() at evals/run_evals.py:255-258 unconditionally deletes and recreates destination \u2014 must wrap with model slug path before calling.",
    "_session_key_for() appends sha256[:8] of model name \u2014 benchmark must use same function (import from evals.run_evals) to generate matching keys.",
    "Megaplan init requires --name flag (command[command.index('--name') + 1] in FakeMegaplanRunner). Execute requires --confirm-destructive --user-approved flags.",

For the execute phase, this is unavoidable since you need build_pass and eval_pass. For plan/critique/review, consider whether you actually need a fully prepared workspace or just the .megaplan directory structure.\n\n**Fallback strategy:** If synthetic state bootstrapping proves too brittle for a phase, fall back to running `megaplan init` + prerequisite phases with a cheap model (e.g., gpt-4.1-mini), then swap to the target model for the target phase only. This is slower but guaranteed to produce valid state.\n\n**T2, T3, T4, T7 can all be coded in parallel** since they have no dependencies on each other. T8 depends on T2+T3 being done. T5 depends on all of T1-T4. T6 depends on T5. T9 is the final integration gate."

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Build a lightweight model benchmarking harness that tests multiple LLM models across megaplan phases to find the best model for each role.

Goal: Run 3 representative evals (simple, medium, hard) through each of ~10 models, scoring each model on each phase independently, to produce a matrix of model×phase scores.

What to build:

1. A benchmark runner (evals/benchmark.py) that:
   - Takes a list of models and a list of eval names
   - For each model, runs it through each phase type independently (not full pipeline):
     a. PLAN test: Give the model a plan prompt for each eval. Score: did it return valid JSON with required keys? Did it use web search? Quality of plan (has success criteria, assumptions, concrete steps)?
     b. CRITIQUE test: Give it a plan to critique. Score: valid JSON? Found real issues vs zero flags?
     c. EXECUTE test: Give it a finalized task list. Score: did it use write_file/patch to modify code? Did the build pass? Did it produce valid task_updates JSON?
     d. REVIEW test: Give it execution evidence. Score: valid JSON with verdict and task_verdicts?
   - Each test is independent (not a full pipeline) — we're measuring per-phase capability
   - Tests run in parallel across models (concurrent subprocess calls)
   - Results stored as a matrix: model × phase × eval → score

2. Scoring dimensions per phase:
   - json_valid: 0/1 — did the response parse as valid JSON matching the schema?
   - json_in_content: 0/1 — was the JSON in the content field (not reasoning/think tags)?
   - required_keys: 0/1 — does it have all required keys?
   - tool_use: 0/1 — did it use appropriate tools (file read for plan/critique, file write for execute)?
   - web_search: 0/1 — did it use web_search (bonus for plan/critique)?
   - build_pass: 0/1 — execute only: does npm run build succeed?
   - eval_pass: 0/1 — execute only: do EVAL.ts assertions pass?

3. Models to test (all via OpenRouter):
   - minimax/minimax-m2.7
   - z-ai/glm-5
   - anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6
   - openai/gpt-4.1-mini
   - google/gemini-2.5-flash
   - meta-llama/llama-4-maverick
   - deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3-0324
   - qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b
   - mistralai/mistral-medium-3
   - cohere/command-a

4. Evals to use:
   - Simple: agent-025-prefer-next-link (add 3 Link components)
   - Medium: agent-022-prefer-server-actions (convert to server action pattern)
   - Hard: agent-000-app-router-migration-simple (full Pages→App Router migration)

5. Output: 
   - Console table showing model × phase scores
   - JSON file with full results for analysis
   - Recommendation of best model per phase

6. Parallelism:
   - Run all models concurrently (each in its own subprocess)
   - Each model runs sequentially through the 3 evals × 4 phases = 12 tests
   - Total: ~120 model-phase tests, but only 10 concurrent at a time

The harness should reuse the existing hermes worker infrastructure but bypass the full megaplan pipeline — it calls the hermes worker directly for each phase test with pre-prepared prompts and scores the result.

  "meta_commentary": "Critical execution guidance:\n\n**Fixture generation (T1) is the bottleneck.** Everything else can be coded without fixtures, but T5 and T9 cannot run until real fixtures exist. If you don't have existing successful eval runs to harvest from, you'll need to run 3 full pipelines with Claude Opus first \u2014 budget ~30-60min and real API costs for this.\n\n**State bootstrapping is the riskiest part.** The FakeMegaplanRunner at tests/test_evals/test_run_evals.py:42-57 is the authoritative reference. Key gotchas: (1) _session_key_for() appends a sha256[:8] hash of the model name \u2014 you MUST import this function from evals.run_evals, not reimplement it. (2) State.json lives inside `.megaplan/plans/<plan_name>/` not at repo root. (3) Session files go to $HERMES_HOME/sessions/ \u2014 set HERMES_HOME per-model to avoid collisions.\n\n**HERMES_HOME isolation is critical for parallelism.** Each concurrent model needs its own HERMES_HOME so session files don't collide. Pass this as env var to the megaplan subprocess. The FakeMegaplanRunner reads `os.environ['HERMES_HOME']` directly (line 126).\n\n**prepare_workspace() will clobber parallel runs** if called with the same workspace_dir \u2014 it does `shutil.rmtree(destination)` at line 256-257. The model slug in the path (DECISION-001) only helps if you construct `workspace_dir/<model_slug>` BEFORE calling prepare_workspace, so the eval_name subdirectory is unique per model.\n\n**Execute scoring depends on npm install completing** \u2014 prepare_workspace runs `npm install` + `npm install next@canary` which can take minutes. For the execute phase, this is unavoidable since you need build_pass and eval_pass. For plan/critique/review, consider whether you actually need a fully prepared workspace or just the .megaplan directory structure.\n\n**Fallback strategy:** If synthetic state bootstrapping proves too brittle for a phase, fall back to running `megaplan init` + prerequisite phases with a cheap model (e.g., gpt-4.1-mini), then swap to the target model for the target phase only. This is slower but guaranteed to produce valid state.\n\n**T2, T3, T4, T7 can all be coded in parallel** since they have no dependencies on each other. T8 depends on T2+T3 being done. T5 depends on all of T1-T4. T6 depends on T5. T9 is the final integration gate."
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Build a lightweight model benchmarking harness that tests multiple LLM models across megaplan phases to find the best model for each role.

Goal: Run 3 representative evals (simple, medium, hard) through each of ~10 models, scoring each model on each phase independently, to produce a matrix of model×phase scores.

What to build:

1. A benchmark runner (evals/benchmark.py) that:
   - Takes a list of models and a list of eval names
   - For each model, runs it through each phase type independently (not full pipeline):
     a. PLAN test: Give the model a plan prompt for each eval. Score: did it return valid JSON with required keys? Did it use web search? Quality of plan (has success criteria, assumptions, concrete steps)?
     b. CRITIQUE test: Give it a plan to critique. Score: valid JSON? Found real issues vs zero flags?
     c. EXECUTE test: Give it a finalized task list. Score: did it use write_file/patch to modify code? Did the build pass? Did it produce valid task_updates JSON?
     d. REVIEW test: Give it execution evidence. Score: valid JSON with verdict and task_verdicts?
   - Each test is independent (not a full pipeline) — we're measuring per-phase capability
   - Tests run in parallel across models (concurrent subprocess calls)
   - Results stored as a matrix: model × phase × eval → score

2. Scoring dimensions per phase:
   - json_valid: 0/1 — did the response parse as valid JSON matching the schema?
   - json_in_content: 0/1 — was the JSON in the content field (not reasoning/think tags)?
   - required_keys: 0/1 — does it have all required keys?
   - tool_use: 0/1 — did it use appropriate tools (file read for plan/critique, file write for execute)?
   - web_search: 0/1 — did it use web_search (bonus for plan/critique)?
   - build_pass: 0/1 — execute only: does npm run build succeed?
   - eval_pass: 0/1 — execute only: do EVAL.ts assertions pass?

3. Models to test (all via OpenRouter):
   - minimax/minimax-m2.7
   - z-ai/glm-5
   - anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6
   - openai/gpt-4.1-mini
   - google/gemini-2.5-flash
   - meta-llama/llama-4-maverick
   - deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3-0324
   - qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b
   - mistralai/mistral-medium-3
   - cohere/command-a

4. Evals to use:
   - Simple: agent-025-prefer-next-link (add 3 Link components)
   - Medium: agent-022-prefer-server-actions (convert to server action pattern)
   - Hard: agent-000-app-router-migration-simple (full Pages→App Router migration)

5. Output: 
   - Console table showing model × phase scores
   - JSON file with full results for analysis
   - Recommendation of best model per phase

6. Parallelism:
   - Run all models concurrently (each in its own subprocess)
   - Each model runs sequentially through the 3 evals × 4 phases = 12 tests
   - Total: ~120 model-phase tests, but only 10 concurrent at a time

The harness should reuse the existing hermes worker infrastructure but bypass the full megaplan pipeline — it calls the hermes worker directly for each phase test with pre-prepared prompts and scores the result.

  "meta_commentary": "Critical execution guidance:\n\n**Fixture generation (T1) is the bottleneck.** Everything else can be coded without fixtures, but T5 and T9 cannot run until real fixtures exist. If you don't have existing successful eval runs to harvest from, you'll need to run 3 full pipelines with Claude Opus first \u2014 budget ~30-60min and real API costs for this.\n\n**State bootstrapping is the riskiest part.** The FakeMegaplanRunner at tests/test_evals/test_run_evals.py:42-57 is the authoritative reference. Key gotchas: (1) _session_key_for() appends a sha256[:8] hash of the model name \u2014 you MUST import this function from evals.run_evals, not reimplement it. (2) State.json lives inside `.megaplan/plans/<plan_name>/` not at repo root. (3) Session files go to $HERMES_HOME/sessions/ \u2014 set HERMES_HOME per-model to avoid collisions.\n\n**HERMES_HOME isolation is critical for parallelism.** Each concurrent model needs its own HERMES_HOME so session files don't collide. Pass this as env var to the megaplan subprocess. The FakeMegaplanRunner reads `os.environ['HERMES_HOME']` directly (line 126).\n\n**prepare_workspace() will clobber parallel runs** if called with the same workspace_dir \u2014 it does `shutil.rmtree(destination)` at line 256-257. The model slug in the path (DECISION-001) only helps if you construct `workspace_dir/<model_slug>` BEFORE calling prepare_workspace, so the eval_name subdirectory is unique per model.\n\n**Execute scoring depends on npm install completing** \u2014 prepare_workspace runs `npm install` + `npm install next@canary` which can take minutes. For the execute phase, this is unavoidable since you need build_pass and eval_pass. For plan/critique/review, consider whether you actually need a fully prepared workspace or just the .megaplan directory structure.\n\n**Fallback strategy:** If synthetic state bootstrapping proves too brittle for a phase, fall back to running `megaplan init` + prerequisite phases with a cheap model (e.g., gpt-4.1-mini), then swap to the target model for the target phase only. This is slower but guaranteed to produce valid state.\n\n**T2, T3, T4, T7 can all be coded in parallel** since they have no dependencies on each other. T8 depends on T2+T3 being done.
